# MillOS place-led beautification, 2026-10-10

## Goal
Complete the approved MillOS beauty, charm and cohesion programme in five place-led batches, using current main as the source baseline and preserving concurrent work. Maintain the British/Dutch miniature-village character, recognisable improved v0.30 civic landmarks, curvy white-and-blue Neuschwanstein castle with ground-accessible stairs, and a believable working mill connected to its community.

1. Establish current matched visual baselines and an asset inventory, including identifiable Aster contributions. Use existing concept art, authored asset tooling, shared materials, surface treatment and capture utilities. Confirm that every changed module ships. Treat older screenshots as references until current captures confirm a gap.
2. Complete the square and bakery as one composition: refined entrances, window recesses and cosy economical interiors, legible signs and convincing displays, purposeful planting, warm local light and grounded furnishings. Keep bakery supply responses tied to verified physical deliveries. Replace completed introductory guidance with useful current objectives or shift outcomes and avoid HUD overlap.
3. Refine homes and civic life: meaningful house archetypes, cared-for gardens, walls, planters and window boxes; church, school and pub identity; coherent paths and inhabited windows. Improve movement and resting contact using existing community schedules. Preserve the fountain bird, scarecrow, consent and safety.
4. Refine canal, farm and landscape: boat and bridge detail, moorings, pond coping, connected water/banks/reeds, field boundaries and natural vegetation. Integrate foreground terrain, wooded foothills and snowy distant mountains with depth and clear silhouettes. Preserve navigation and drainage continuity.
5. Refine castle and arrivals: harmonious towers, roofs, balconies, masonry and glazing; elegant white/blue curves; usable stairs and settings. Improve forecourt, shelter and signs, road joins and night legibility.
6. Refine factory and play: coherent fittings, sacks, pallets, vehicles and service details without breaking batches. Make the order-to-dispatch loop understandable, with visible state, blockers, actions and consequences. Use existing campaign incidents for gently educational choices and clear shift feedback; never manufacture inventory, fulfilled orders or safety authority.
7. Unify day, dusk, night and rain: graduated sky and afterglow in the real sun direction, layered clouds, atmospheric mountains, sky-responsive water, local warm pools and readable night routes. Use one celestial shadow caster, bounded local lights, shared geometry/materials, instancing and existing LOD. Preserve genuine scene-ready loading and original graphics budgets.
8. Verify each coherent batch with existing focused checks and a batched visual review, correcting confirmed problems. Run required final typecheck, lint, formatting and production build, plus relevant gameplay tests and representative rendered views. Report busy-host performance separately from regressions; retain the original Medium >=60 average FPS, <=16.7ms p95 and >=45 FPS 1% low and unchanged Low budgets. No audio or music.
Completion requires actual source changes across all five batches, passing applicable gates, matched runtime evidence, a finite acceptance review and an honest account of any external limitations. Keep changes local unless separately authorised to publish; preserve unrelated processes, assets, caches and worktrees.

## Native remote admission diagnosis
Three capped tiny probes completed and closed normally, without uploading the game. NVIDIA A10 Vulkan reads correct pixels, but normal full Chromium reports GPU sandboxed:false. Early sandbox activation is real in the retained log, followed by Permission denied opening the bundled libGLESv2.so and GPU initialization exit. This is a resource-load ordering failure, not evidence that Modal lacks seccomp. The status page confirms namespace/seccomp capability, but cannot certify the failed GPU process.

One discriminating resource-preload probe follows the exact Chromium143 startup contract: load the two trusted bundled ANGLE libraries before early GPU sandbox activation, retaining the fatal-failure setting and every native gate. No file-broker policy, sandbox check or game setting is changed. Three failure modes remain: later lazy dependencies may still be denied, library constructors may create threads too early, or the driver may need a denied device/ICD path. Any native failure retains a red admission result; no game payload is sent until real sandboxed hardware is proven. Working if CDP reports sandboxed:true, an out-of-process hardware renderer and valid actual pixels.

## Proven truck first-use stalls and delivered correction
A trace of the actual first moving sample found six native GetProgramiv waits coincident with the slow frames. A warmed follow-up, ordinary getProgramParameter wrapper and draw/uniform wrappers did not identify those waits. The discriminating getProgramInfoLog wrapper did: six newly linked programs blocked for roughly15 to35ms. Immediate owner snapshots identify the full truck's detailed frame/tractor/trailer, tyres and clearcoated paint, rather than personnel. The installed Three implementation initializes its first-use metadata through that API; no shader diagnostics were disabled.

TruckVisualLOD previously unmounted the detailed truck on distance crossings and disposed its finishes. Both existing models now remain mounted, with the original120/135m hysteresis and15-frame cadence selecting exactly one visible subtree. Animation callbacks remain active in both. Retention alone did not remove first-use compilation for finishes that had never rendered under the complete lighting rig.

StartupReadiness therefore prepares only the two detailed truck subtrees, after actual assets and static batches finish, inside the real main-scene onBeforeRender callback. This preserves the actual composer's render target, camera, environment and lighting. Exact program variants are captured, readiness polled, and Three's checked first-use metadata initialized behind the overlay.
